(wide/smooth/round). Order the subtasks so the grasp comes first.\\n- [Release wide held objects once they overlap the container edge.](global/pitched-side-grasp-overedge-release.md) \\u2014 A wide bowl or cup collides before reaching a deep drawer or cavity target, but the predicate can accept an over-edge drop into the container.\\n- [Order multi-place tasks so nothing is carried or re-grasped over an Hmm, but is x=0.02 too far forward relative to the basket center? The rim front top was at x=0.091 and back rim at x=-0.047. If the rim is the top opening, center x = 0.022. But wait — those rim tops were computed at different image rows with different z; the values (0.091, 0.141) and (-0.047, 0.126) look plausible as actual rim top points. However, I worry: is the basket's front rim maybe at x=0.09 and back at -0.05, giving a depth of 0.14 — but the basket looks like ~0.10x0.10 externally in y (0.19-0.36 = 0.17!). Hmm, y span 0.17 is large.

Wait, maybe I'm over-estimating. Let me re-derive from the agentview at step 5: basket's white rim: left edge col ~718, right edge col ~960. At the table-plane mapping y = -0.0096 + (col-500)*0.000799 (from col 500→-0.0096, col 950→0.3499: slope = 0.3595/450 = 0.000799). At the rim height (z~0.13), the mapping is different, but roughly y_left ≈ -0.0096+218*0.0008 = 0.165, y_right = -0.0096+460*0.0008 = 0.358. So basket spans y from ~0.17 to ~0.36, i.e., 19cm wide. Hmm, that's big for a LIBERO basket, but these baskets (the "basket" object in LIBERO object suite) are fairly large. Actually the standard LIBERO basket is ~15cm. OK.

Center y ≈ 0.265.

Rather than guessing, once over the basket I can use the wrist camera and back-project the cavity corners, which is much more reliable. Let's move to (0.02, 0.265, 0.38) and inspect the wrist.
